\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}a_{ij}(\mathbf{x})\partial^{2}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}b_{i}(\mathbf{x})\partial_{i},

(2.138)

\displaystyle\tilde{L}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\tilde{a}_{ij}(\mathbf{x})\partial^{2}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\tilde{b}_{i}(\mathbf{x})\partial_{i},

(2.139)

be two differential operators. We say that
\tilde{L}=L+O(\varepsilon(\mathbf{x}))
if

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{ij}=(1+O(\varepsilon(\mathbf{x})))a_{ij},\quad\tilde{b}_{i}=(1+O(\varepsilon(\mathbf{x})))b_{i},

(2.140)

for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant m.
Similarly, we say that
\tilde{L}=L\cdot e^{O(\varepsilon(\mathbf{x}))}
if

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{ij}=e^{O(\varepsilon(\mathbf{x}))}a_{ij},\quad\tilde{b}_{i}=e^{O(\varepsilon(\mathbf{x}))}b_{i}.

(2.141)

Lemma 2.28
.

Recall the local holomorphic chart
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
introduced after Lemma
2.23
. Assume that in this chart a Kähler metric
g
is given by

\displaystyle g_{i\bar{j}}=-F\varphi_{i\bar{j}}+G\left(\varphi_{i}-\frac{\delta_{in}}{z_{n}}\right)\left(\varphi_{\bar{j}}-\frac{\delta_{jn}}{\overline{z}_{n}}\right),

(2.142)

where
F,G
are positive functions and
\varphi
is a function of
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n-1}
. Then its inverse
g^{\bar{j}k}
is given by

\displaystyle g^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}=-\frac{\varphi^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}}{F}\;\,(1\leqslant\alpha,\beta\leqslant n-1),\;\,g^{\bar{\alpha}n}=-\frac{\varphi^{\bar{\alpha}\gamma}\varphi_{\gamma}z_{n}}{F},\;\,g^{\bar{n}n}=\frac{|z_{n}|^{2}(F-G\varphi^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}\varphi_{\alpha}\varphi_{\bar{\beta}})}{FG}.

(2.143)

The next corollary follows from Proposition
2.26
.

Corollary 2.29
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and let
\eta\in(0,\delta]
.
Then at
t=\eta T
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\omega_{T}}=(L_{h}+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau))\cdot e^{O_{\delta}(\eta^{n+1})},

(2.144)

where the cuspidal Laplacian
L_{h}
was introduced in
[
13
, Lemma 2.5]
. Using the notation
x=-1/t
and
\theta={\rm arg}\,z_{n}
from
[
13
]
, an explicit formula for
L_{h}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}L_{h}u=\frac{1}{n+1}(x^{2}u_{xx}+(n+1)xu_{x}-(n+1)u-x^{-1}\varphi^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}u_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+(2x)^{-1}u_{\theta\theta})+F,\\
F=F(x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha},x^{-1}u_{\theta\alpha},x^{-1}u_{\theta\bar{\alpha}},x^{-1}u_{\theta\theta}),\end{split}

(2.145)

with
F
smooth in
x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha}
and linear homogeneous in the other arguments. In addition, for all
k\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle|\nabla_{\omega_{cusp}}^{k}(\omega_{T}-\omega_{cusp})|_{\omega_{cusp}}=O_{\delta,k}(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}|\tau|+\eta^{n+1}).

(2.146)

Proof.

(
2.144
) follows from Proposition
2.26
, Lemma
2.28
, and the proof of Lemma 2.5 in
[
13
]
.

To prove (
2.146
), we first rewrite (
2.106
) as

\displaystyle\psi_{T}^{\prime\prime}=(n+1)^{n}e^{\psi_{T}-\psi_{cusp}}(-t)^{-n-1}(\psi_{T}^{\prime})^{1-n}

(2.147)

and differentiate it
multiple times to inductively show that

\displaystyle\psi^{(k)}_{T}(\eta T)

\displaystyle=\psi_{cusp}^{(k)}(\eta T)(1+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(\eta^{n+1})).

(2.148)

In fact, cases
k=1,2
follow directly from Proposition
2.26
.
For
k\geqslant 3
, when we differentiate (
2.147
)
k-2
times using the product rule, every term on the right-hand side is of the form

\displaystyle(-t)^{-k}(C+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(\eta^{n+1})).

(2.149)

By formally writing
1=e^{\psi_{cusp}-\psi_{cusp}}
and differentiating

\displaystyle\psi_{cusp}^{\prime\prime}=(n+1)^{n}e^{\psi_{cusp}-\psi_{cusp}}(-t)^{-n-1}(\psi_{cusp}^{\prime})^{1-n}

(2.150)

k-2
times, we know that the constants
C
add up to
(n+1)(k-1)!
as

\displaystyle\psi_{cusp}^{(k)}(t)=(n+1)(k-1)!(-t)^{-k}.

(2.151)

Here we use the fact that the
(k-2)
-th derivatives of (
2.147
) and
(k-2)
-th derivatives of (
2.150
) are in similar forms.
With this, we derive (
2.148
). Near a point
q
with
t_{*}:=t(q)\in[\delta T,\tau]
, we then consider quasi-coordinates for
\omega_{cusp}
as in (
2.109
):

\displaystyle(\check{x}_{\alpha},\check{y}_{\alpha},\check{x},\check{\theta}):=((-t_{*})^{-\frac{1}{2}}x_{\alpha},(-t_{*})^{-\frac{1}{2}}y_{\alpha},-t_{*}x,(-t_{*})^{-1}\theta).

(2.152)

Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
2.23
, we need to show that
the
C^{k}
norm of

\displaystyle\frac{\psi^{\prime}_{T}-\psi^{\prime}_{cusp}}{\psi^{\prime}_{cusp}},\;\,\frac{\psi^{\prime\prime}_{T}-\psi^{\prime\prime}_{cusp}}{\psi^{\prime\prime}_{cusp}}

(2.153)

with respect to (
2.152
) on
\{2t_{*}<t<\frac{1}{2}t_{*}\}
is bounded by
C_{k}(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}|\tau|+\eta^{n+1})
.
For
k=0
, this follows directly from (
2.148
). For
k\geqslant 1
, we prove it by induction by applying (
2.116
)–(
2.118
).
∎

Proposition 2.30
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be a small dimensional constant. Define the coordinate
s:=1-t/T
and restrict it to
s\in[2|T|^{\alpha-1},\delta]
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then
\Delta_{\omega_{T}}
is of the form

\displaystyle\Delta_{\omega_{T}}=L+O(|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}s^{-\frac{1}{n}})\cdot\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}}).

(2.154)

Here the
O
notation is understood in the sense of Definition
2.27
with respect to the local chart
s,\theta,z_{\alpha}
and the model operator
L
takes the following form:

\displaystyle\begin{split}Lu&=\left(\frac{n+1}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{n}}c(n)^{-\frac{n+1}{n}}(ns^{\frac{n-1}{n}}u_{ss}+(n-1)s^{-\frac{1}{n}}u_{s})+|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}s^{-\frac{1}{n}}\mathcal{T}u\\
&+C|b|^{-\frac{2}{n+1}}s^{\frac{n-1}{n}}u_{\theta\theta}+s^{-\frac{1}{n}}\mathcal{H}u,\end{split}

(2.155)

where
C>0
is a constant,
\mathcal{T}
is an elliptic operator on
E
and
\mathcal{H}
is linear homogeneous in

\displaystyle u_{s\alpha},u_{s\bar{\beta}},u_{s\theta},|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}u_{\alpha\theta},|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}u_{\bar{\beta}\theta}

(2.156)

with coefficients that are smooth in
x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha}
and independent of
b,T
.

The term
O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}})
in (
2.154
) stands for a comparison of differential operators as in (
2.140
), so for applications it is important to know that this term is
>-1
. This is ensured by our assumption
s\leqslant\delta
. Similarly, given the condition
s\geqslant 2|T|^{\alpha-1}
, we can observe that the term with
\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}
in (
2.154
) is

\displaystyle O(|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}s^{-\frac{1}{n}})\cdot\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}=O(|b|^{\frac{\alpha}{n+1}})\cdot|b|^{-\frac{2}{n+1}}s^{\frac{n-1}{n}}u_{\theta\theta}.

(2.157)

Hence, this term represents a higher-order contribution compared to the operator
L.

Proof of Proposition
2.30
.

By (
2.130
) and (
2.89
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)}(e^{\xi}-1)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}d\xi&=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}(1-\eta)|T|\\
&=(c(n)+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau))(1-\eta),\end{split}

(2.158)

where
1-\eta=s
and the
O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)
term is independent of
s.

When
\xi>0
is bounded,

\displaystyle(e^{\xi}-1)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}=\xi^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}(1+O(\xi))=\xi^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}+O(\xi^{\frac{n}{n+1}}).

(2.159)

Thus, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)}(e^{\xi}-1)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}d\xi=\frac{n+1}{n}({\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)})^{\frac{n}{n+1}}(1+O({\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)})).

(2.160)

By (
2.158
),

\displaystyle\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)=\left(\frac{n}{n+1}\right)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}\left(c(n)+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)\right)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}s^{\frac{n+1}{n}}(1+O(\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T))),

(2.161)

which further implies that

\displaystyle\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)=\left(\frac{n}{n+1}\right)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}\left(c(n)+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)\right)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}s^{\frac{n+1}{n}}(1+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}})).

(2.162)

The metric
\omega_{T}
is given by

\displaystyle\omega_{T}=-\psi_{T}^{\prime}i\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi+\psi_{T}^{\prime\prime}i(-\partial\varphi+\partial\log z_{n})\wedge(-\overline{\partial}\varphi+\overline{\partial}\log z_{n}).

(2.163)

Thanks to (
2.162
), we can evaluate the coefficients as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{T}^{\prime}(\eta T)&=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}(e^{\psi_{T}(\eta T)+a}+b)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\\
&=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}(e^{\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)}-1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\\
&=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\left(\frac{n}{n+1}\right)^{\frac{1}{n}}|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}s^{\frac{1}{n}}\left(c(n)^{\frac{1}{n}}+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}})\right),\end{split}

(2.164)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{T}^{\prime\prime}(\eta T)&=\frac{e^{\psi_{T}(\eta T)+a}}{(\psi_{T}^{\prime}(\eta T))^{n-1}}=\frac{|b|e^{\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)}}{(\psi_{T}^{\prime}(\eta T))^{n-1}}\\
&=(n+1)^{\frac{1-n}{n+1}}\left(\frac{n}{n+1}\right)^{\frac{1-n}{n}}|b|^{\frac{2}{n+1}}s^{\frac{1-n}{n}}\left(c(n)^{\frac{1-n}{n}}+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}})\right).\end{split}

(2.165)

Setting
x=-1/t
, we have
x=(1-s)^{-1}|T|^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\partial_{x}

\displaystyle=|T|(1-s)^{2}\partial_{s},\quad\partial_{xx}^{2}=|T|^{2}(1-s)^{4}\partial_{s}^{2}-2|T|^{2}(1-s)^{3}\partial_{s}.

(2.166)

Given this conversion formula, it will be enough to express
\Delta_{\omega_{T}}
in terms of
\partial_{x},\partial^{2}_{xx}
. This can be done by a computation similar to the proof of
[
13
, Lemma 2.5]
. Set

\displaystyle Q:=\varphi^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}\varphi_{\alpha}\varphi_{\bar{\beta}},

(2.167)

which is uniformly bounded. Applying Lemma
2.28
with
F=\psi^{\prime}(\eta T)
,
G=\psi^{\prime\prime}(\eta T)
, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}g^{\bar{n}n}\partial_{n}\partial_{\bar{n}}&=\frac{r^{2}(F-GQ)}{FG}\left(\frac{x^{2}}{z_{n}}\partial_{x}-\frac{i}{2z_{n}}\partial_{\theta}\right)\left(\frac{x^{2}}{\overline{z}_{n}}\partial_{x}+\frac{i}{2\overline{z}_{n}}\partial_{\theta}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{1}{G}-\frac{Q}{F}\right)\left(x^{4}\partial^{2}_{xx}+2x^{3}\partial_{x}+\frac{1}{4}\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}\right)\\
&=(n+1)^{\frac{n-1}{n+1}}\left(\frac{n}{n+1}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}c(n)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}|b|^{-\frac{2}{n+1}}s^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\left(x^{4}\partial^{2}_{xx}+2x^{3}\partial_{x}+\frac{1}{4}\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}\right)\\
&-\frac{Q}{F}(x^{4}\partial^{2}_{xx}+2x^{3}\partial_{x})+O(|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}s^{-\frac{1}{n}})\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}})\\
&=(n+1)\left(\frac{n}{n+1}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}c(n)^{-\frac{n+1}{n}}s^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\partial^{2}_{ss}+C|b|^{-\frac{2}{n+1}}s^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}\\
&-\frac{Q}{F}(x^{4}\partial^{2}_{xx}+2x^{3}\partial_{x})+O(|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}s^{-\frac{1}{n}})\partial^{2}_{\theta\theta}+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}}),\end{split}

(2.168)

\displaystyle\begin{split}g^{\bar{\alpha}n}\partial_{n}\partial_{\bar{\alpha}}&=-\frac{\varphi^{\bar{\alpha}\gamma}\varphi_{\gamma}z_{n}}{F}\left(\frac{x^{2}}{z_{n}}\partial_{x}-\frac{i}{2z_{n}}\partial_{\theta}\right)(\partial_{\bar{\alpha}}-x^{2}\varphi_{\bar{\alpha}}\partial_{x})\\
&=-\frac{x^{2}}{F}\varphi^{\bar{\alpha}\gamma}\varphi_{\gamma}\partial^{2}_{x\bar{\alpha}}+\frac{Q}{F}(x^{4}\partial^{2}_{xx}+2x^{3}\partial_{x})-\frac{ix^{2}Q\partial^{2}_{x\theta}}{2F}+\frac{i\varphi^{\bar{\alpha}\gamma}\varphi_{\gamma}\partial^{2}_{\bar{\alpha}\theta}}{F}\\
&=s^{-\frac{1}{n}}H_{1}(x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha},\partial^{2}_{s\bar{\alpha}},|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\partial^{2}_{ss},|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\partial_{s},\partial^{2}_{s\theta},|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}\partial^{2}_{\bar{\alpha}\theta})+O(b^{n+1}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}}),\end{split}

(2.169)

\displaystyle\begin{split}g^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\bar{\beta}}&=-\frac{\varphi^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}}{F}(\partial_{z_{\alpha}}-x^{2}\varphi_{\alpha}\partial_{x})(\partial^{2}_{\bar{\beta}}-x^{2}\varphi_{\bar{\beta}}\partial_{x})\\
&=-\frac{\varphi^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}}{F}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}+(n-1)\frac{x^{2}\partial_{x}}{F}+\frac{x^{2}\varphi^{\bar{\beta}\alpha}(\varphi_{\alpha}\partial^{2}_{x\bar{\beta}}+\varphi_{\bar{\beta}}\partial^{2}_{x\alpha})}{F}-\frac{Q(x^{4}\partial^{2}_{xx}+2x^{3}\partial_{x})}{F}\\
&=|b|^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}s^{-\frac{1}{n}}T+(n-1)\left(\frac{n}{n+1}\right)^{-\frac{1}{n}}c(n)^{-\frac{n+1}{n}}s^{-\frac{1}{n}}\partial_{s}\\
&+s^{-\frac{1}{n}}H_{2}(x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha},\partial^{2}_{s\alpha},\partial^{2}_{s\bar{\beta}},|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\partial^{2}_{ss},|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\partial_{s})+O(b^{n+1}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}}),\end{split}

(2.170)

where
H_{1},H_{2}
are smooth in
x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha}
and linear homogeneous with respect to their differential operator arguments.
As
\Delta_{\omega_{T}}
is a real operator, all terms with an
i
must cancel out. In addition, the terms

\displaystyle\frac{Q}{F}(x^{4}\partial^{2}_{xx}+2x^{3}\partial_{x})

(2.171)

in (
2.168
)–(
2.170
) cancel out. So the terms
|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\partial^{2}_{ss},|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\partial_{s}
in
H_{1},H_{2}
cancel out as well.
∎

Let us also note the following formulas for later use. By repeatedly differentiating (
2.164
)–(
2.165
), we may prove inductively that, when
k>2
, there are some constants
C_{j,l}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi^{(k)}_{T}(\eta T)=&\sum_{j=-(k-2)}^{n(k-2)}\sum_{l=1}^{k-1}\frac{C_{j,l}e^{l(\psi_{T}(\eta T)+a)}}{(\psi^{\prime}_{T}(\eta T))^{n-1+j}}\\
=&\sum_{j=-(k-2)}^{n(k-2)}\sum_{l=1}^{k-1}\frac{C_{j,l}|b|^{l}e^{l(\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T))}}{(\psi^{\prime}_{T}(\eta T))^{n-1+j}}\\
=&\sum_{j=-(k-2)}^{n(k-2)}\sum_{l=1}^{k-1}C_{n,j,l}|b|^{l-\frac{n-1+j}{n+1}}s^{\frac{1-n-j}{n}}\left(c(n)^{\frac{1-n}{n}}+O(|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}\tau)+O(s^{\frac{n+1}{n}})\right).\end{split}

(2.172)

The following lemma about the shape of the volume form of
\omega_{T}
is also an easy consequence of the computations in this section.

Lemma 2.31
.

Write
s=1-\frac{t}{T}
as before. Then for every
\eta\in(0,1)
we have that

\displaystyle\omega_{T}^{n}|_{t=\eta T}=|T|^{-n}\mu_{T}(1-\eta)\,ds\wedge d\theta\wedge d{\rm Vol}_{E},

(2.173)

where the radial volume density
\mu_{T}:(0,1)\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

There are constants
e(n),e^{\prime}(n)>0
with
e(n)\leqslant\mu_{T}(1-\eta)\leqslant e^{\prime}(n)\eta^{-(n+1)}
for all
\eta,T
.

(2)

\mu_{\infty}:=\lim_{T\to-\infty}\mu_{T}
exists pointwise and is smooth.

(3)

\mu_{\infty}(s)\to const>0
as
s\to 0^{+}
and
\eta^{n+1}\mu_{\infty}(1-\eta)\to const>0
as
\eta\to 0^{+}
.

Proof.

From the Kähler-Einstein equation, it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\omega_{T}^{n}|_{t=\eta T}=e^{\prime\prime}(n)|T|^{-n}e^{\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)}ds\wedge d\theta\wedge d{\rm Vol}_{E}

(2.174)

for some constant
e^{\prime\prime}(n)>0
. For item (1), we use (
2.120
) to estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}0\leqslant\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)&\leqslant\psi_{cusp}(\eta T)+C-\psi_{T}(T)\\
&\leqslant-(n+1)\log\eta-(n+1)\log(-T)+C-\psi_{T}(T)\\
&\leqslant-(n+1)\log\eta+C.\end{split}

(2.175)

For items (2) and (3), we use the more precise formula (
2.127
), which is valid for all
\eta\in(0,1)
. This tells us that
\psi_{T}(\eta T)-\psi_{T}(T)
has a pointwise limit as
T\to-\infty
, which is a smooth function of
\eta
and which moreover differs from
-(n+1)\log\eta+C
by
O(\eta^{n+1})
as
\eta\to 0^{+}
. This also establishes the limit as
\eta\to 0^{+}
in item (3). For the limit as
s\to 0^{+}
in item (3), we can instead look at (
2.162
).
∎

2.4.3.
Estimates asymptotically close to the Tian-Yau end (orange region)

Recall from Remark
2.22
that the orange gluing region takes the form
t\in[T+T_{0},T+2T_{0}]
for some positive
T_{0}
depending on
\sigma
such that
T_{0}\to+\infty
but
T_{0}/T\rightarrow 0^{-}
as
b\rightarrow 0^{-}
. From now on we fix an
\alpha\in(0,1)
and set

\displaystyle T_{0}:=|T|^{\alpha}.

(2.176)

In this section we will prove decay estimates for the difference of the Tian-Yau metric and the neck metric
\omega_{T}
in the orange region. We slightly abuse notation by writing

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathcal{C}}(t)=\frac{n}{n+1}(t-T)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}

(2.177)

for the Calabi model potential shifted by
T
. We also set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}:=n^{\frac{1}{n}}.

(2.178)

We wish to glue
\omega_{T}
with
\mathfrak{c}|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
. To this end, we introduce the error term

\displaystyle E(t):=\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)-\mathfrak{c}|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\psi_{\mathcal{C}}.

(2.179)

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\psi_{T}(T)}^{\psi_{T}(t)}(e^{\xi+a}+b)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}d\xi=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}(t-T).

(2.180)

Taking
s=\xi-\psi_{T}(T)
, we have that for
t-T\in[T_{0},2T_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)}(e^{s}-1)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}ds=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}(t-T)=O(T_{0}|T|^{-1}).

(2.181)

Then
\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)=o(1)
as
T\rightarrow-\infty.
When
s
is small,

\displaystyle(e^{s}-1)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}=s^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}+O(s^{\frac{n}{n+1}}).

(2.182)

We have that

\frac{n+1}{n}(\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T))^{\frac{n}{n+1}}(1+O(\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)))=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}(t-T).

(2.183)

In sum, for
t-T\in[T_{0},2T_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)=\mathfrak{c}|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\psi_{\mathcal{C}}+O(|b|^{\frac{2}{n}}T_{0}^{\frac{2(n+1)}{n}}),

(2.184)

which shows that

\displaystyle E(t)=O(|b|^{\frac{2}{n}}T_{0}^{\frac{2(n+1)}{n}}).

(2.185)

We can actually expand
E(t)
in terms of powers of
|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\psi_{\mathcal{C}}
up to any finite order.
For example,

\displaystyle E(t)=\frac{\mathfrak{c}^{2}}{4n+2}|b|^{\frac{2}{n}}\psi_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}+O(|b|^{\frac{3}{n}}\psi_{\mathcal{C}}^{3})\;\,\text{as}\;\,|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\psi_{\mathcal{C}}\to 0.

(2.186)

More precisely, we have the following statement.

Lemma 2.32
.

E(t)
is an analytic function of
|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}(t-T)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}
at
|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}(t-T)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}=0.

Proof.

Applying the Maclaurin series of
e^{s}
,

\displaystyle(e^{s}-1)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}=s^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{s^{i}}{(i+1)!}\right)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}=s^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{i}s^{i},

(2.187)

where
a_{0}=1
and
C^{i}a_{i}\rightarrow 0
for some
C>0
. Indeed,
\left(\frac{e^{s}-1}{s}\right)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}
is analytic at
s=0.

By (
2.181
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{i}(\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T))^{i+\frac{n}{n+1}}=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}|b|^{\frac{1}{n+1}}(t-T),

(2.188)

where again
\tilde{a}_{0}>0
and
C^{i}\tilde{a}_{i}\rightarrow 0
for some
C>0
. Taking the
\frac{n+1}{n}
-th power of both sides, we get

\displaystyle(\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T))\left(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\tilde{a}_{i}(\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T))^{i}\right)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}=(n+1)^{\frac{1}{n}}|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}(t-T)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}.

(2.189)

The left-hand side is obviously analytic in
\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)
around
\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)=0
.
Then the lemma follows from the Lagrange inversion theorem.
∎

Lastly, we estimate the derivatives of
i\partial\overline{\partial}E
under the scaled Calabi metric
|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
. For this we need one more lemma establishing quasi-coordinates for this metric. Following our work after Lemma
2.23
, we define real coordinates on the universal cover of the annulus
\{y:=t-T\in[T_{0},2T_{0}]\}
via

\displaystyle(\check{x}_{\alpha},\check{y}_{\alpha},\check{y},\check{\theta}):=(T_{0}^{\frac{1}{2n}}x_{\alpha},T_{0}^{\frac{1}{2n}}y_{\alpha},T_{0}^{\frac{1-n}{2n}}y,T_{0}^{\frac{1-n}{2n}}\theta).

(2.190)

We also introduce the corresponding holomorphic coordinates, with
w=\log z_{n}
:

\displaystyle(\check{z}_{\alpha},\check{w}):=(T_{0}^{\frac{1}{2n}}z_{\alpha},T_{0}^{\frac{1-n}{2n}}w).

(2.191)

Lemma 2.33
.

The un-scaled Calabi metric
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
is uniformly equivalent to
(\delta_{jk})
under (
2.190
)–(
2.191
). For all
k\geqslant 1
its entries under (
2.190
)–(
2.191
) satisfy a uniform
C^{k}
bound independent of
T,T_{0}
.

Proof.

Because
w=\log z_{n}
, we locally have that

\displaystyle t=\log h=-\varphi+w+\overline{w}.

(2.192)

This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega_{\mathcal{C}}=\frac{n}{n+1}i\partial\overline{\partial}(t-T)^{\frac{n+1}{n}}&=\frac{1}{n}(t-T)^{\frac{1-n}{n}}i\partial t\wedge\overline{\partial}t+(t-T)^{\frac{1}{n}}i\partial\overline{\partial}t\\
&=\frac{1}{n}(t-T)^{\frac{1-n}{n}}i\partial(-\varphi+w)\wedge\overline{\partial}(-\varphi+\overline{w})-(t-T)^{\frac{1}{n}}i\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi,\end{split}

(2.193)

where
\varphi
is a quadratic polynomial in
z_{\alpha},\overline{z}_{\alpha}
for
\alpha=1,\ldots,n-1
.
Under the coordinates

(\check{z}_{\alpha},\check{w})
given in (
2.191
), it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega_{\mathcal{C}}&=\frac{1}{n}((t-T)T_{0}^{-1})^{\frac{1-n}{n}}i(-T_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{z_{\alpha}}d\check{z}_{\alpha}+d\check{w})\wedge(-T_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{\overline{z}_{\alpha}}d\overline{{\check{z}}}_{\alpha}+d\overline{\check{w}})\\
&-((t-T)T_{0}^{-1})^{\frac{1}{n}}\varphi_{z_{\alpha}\overline{z}_{\beta}}id\check{z}_{\alpha}\wedge d\overline{\check{z}}_{\beta}.\end{split}

(2.194)

This is uniformly equivalent to the Euclidean metric in the chart (
2.191
) for
y=t-T\in[T_{0},2T_{0}]
. To check the desired
C^{k}
bound for
k=1
, note that
\varphi_{z_{\alpha}\overline{z}_{\beta}}
is a constant, that
\varphi_{\overline{z}_{\alpha}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\varphi_{\overline{z}_{\alpha}}}{\partial\check{z}_{\beta}}=T_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\frac{\partial\varphi_{\overline{z}_{\alpha}}}{\partial z_{\beta}}=T_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2n}}\varphi_{z_{\beta}\overline{z}_{\alpha}},

(2.195)

and that, by (
2.192
),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial((t-T)T_{0}^{-1})}{\partial\check{w}}=T_{0}^{\frac{n-1}{2n}}\frac{\partial((t-T)T_{0}^{-1})}{\partial w}=T_{0}^{-\frac{n+1}{2n}}.

(2.196)

Thus, the first coordinate derivatives of
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
are uniformly decaying. This pattern persists for all
k
.
∎

Proposition 2.34
.

For
t\in[T+T_{0},T+2T_{0}]
and
j=1,2,

\left|\nabla^{j}_{|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\omega_{\mathcal{C}}}(i\partial\overline{\partial}E)\right|^{2}_{|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\omega_{\mathcal{C}}}\leqslant C|b|^{\frac{2-j}{n}}T_{0}^{\frac{(2-j)(n+1)}{n}}.

(2.197)

Proof.

Define
y:=t-T
. By deriving the Taylor expansion of
\psi_{T}(t)-\psi_{T}(T)
with respect to
|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}y^{\frac{n+1}{n}}
using (
2.183
),
we obtain that for
y\in[T_{0},2T_{0}]
and
j\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle|(y\partial_{t})^{j}E(t)|\leqslant C_{j}|b|^{\frac{2}{n}}T_{0}^{\frac{2(n+1)}{n}},

(2.198)

where the constants
C_{j}
are independent of
b
.

Recall the real chart
(\check{x}_{\alpha},\check{y}_{\alpha},\check{y},\check{\theta})
and the holomorphic chart
(\check{z}_{\alpha},\check{w})
on the universal cover of the annulus
\{y\in[T_{0},2T_{0}]\}
defined in (
2.190
)–(
2.191
) above. By Lemma
2.33
, these are quasi-coordinates for
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
, i.e., the pullback of
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
to the universal cover is uniformly smoothly comparable to the Euclidean metric in these coordinates and its
C^{k}
norms are bounded independently of
T_{0}
.

Now we are ready to estimate the derivatives of
i\partial\overline{\partial}E
. In fact,

\displaystyle\begin{split}i\partial\overline{\partial}E=E^{\prime}i\partial\overline{\partial}t+E^{\prime\prime}i\partial t\wedge\overline{\partial}t=-E^{\prime}i\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi+E^{\prime\prime}i(\partial\varphi-\partial w)\wedge(\overline{\partial}\varphi-\overline{\partial}\overline{w}),\end{split}

(2.199)

where

\displaystyle T_{0}^{\frac{1}{n}}i\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi,\quad T_{0}^{\frac{1-n}{n}}i(\partial\varphi-\partial w)\wedge(\overline{\partial}\varphi-\overline{\partial}\overline{w})

(2.200)

are uniformly smoothly bounded under (
2.191
). So we need to check the regularity of

\displaystyle E^{\prime}/T_{0}^{\frac{1}{n}},\quad E^{\prime\prime}/T_{0}^{\frac{1-n}{n}}.

(2.201)

Because these are radial functions, we just need to check the derivatives of (
2.201
) with respect to
\check{y}
. Applying (
2.198
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial(E^{\prime}/T_{0}^{\frac{1}{n}})}{\partial\check{y}}\right|+\left|\frac{\partial(E^{\prime\prime}/T_{0}^{\frac{1-n}{n}})}{\partial\check{y}}\right|\leqslant C|b|^{\frac{2}{n}}T_{0}^{\frac{n+1}{2n}},

(2.202)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial^{2}(E^{\prime}/T_{0}^{\frac{1}{n}})}{\partial\check{y}^{2}}\right|+\left|\frac{\partial^{2}(E^{\prime\prime}/T_{0}^{\frac{1-n}{n}})}{\partial\check{y}^{2}}\right|\leqslant C|b|^{\frac{2}{n}}.

(2.203)

We conclude that the 2-form
i\partial\overline{\partial}E
satisfies for
j=1,2
that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{j}_{\omega_{\mathcal{C}}}(i\partial\overline{\partial}E)\right|_{\omega_{\mathcal{C}}}\leqslant C|b|^{\frac{2}{n}}T_{0}^{\frac{n+1}{2n}(2-j)}.

(2.204)

Now the metric
|b|^{\frac{1}{n}}\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
in the statement of the proposition is simply a rescaling of
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
. If
\tilde{\omega}=A\omega
for some constant
A>0
, then for any 2-form
\mathbf{T}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla^{j}_{\tilde{\omega}}\mathbf{T}|^{2}_{\tilde{\omega}}=A^{-2-j}|\nabla^{j}_{\omega}\mathbf{T}|^{2}_{\omega}.

(2.205)

The proposition follows from (
2.204
)–(
2.205
).
∎

3.
The glued approximate Kähler-Einstein metric

3.1.
Setting up the glued metric and the Monge-Ampère equation

Before defining the glued metric, we briefly review some material from previous sections and fix some parameters.

First, we have a family of Tian-Yau spaces
TY_{\sigma}
as our singularity models, see (
2.6
). We have smooth embeddings
\Phi_{\sigma}:TY_{0}\setminus\overline{B}_{|\sigma|^{1/3}R}\to TY_{\sigma}
onto a neighborhood of infinity in
TY_{\sigma}
for
\sigma\neq 0
, see (
2.73
). In Section
2.3
we review the Tian-Yau construction
(\psi_{TY_{1}},\omega_{TY_{1}},\Omega_{TY_{1}})
on
TY_{1}
and its decay towards the Calabi model data
(\psi_{\mathcal{C}},\omega_{\mathcal{C}},\Omega_{\mathcal{C}})
on
TY_{0}
via
\Phi_{1}
. This gives rise to a family
(\psi_{TY_{\sigma}},\omega_{TY_{\sigma}},\Omega_{TY_{\sigma}})
via the biholomorphism
m_{\sigma}:TY_{\sigma}\to TY_{1}
of (
2.73
). A tricky point here is that, in the Tian-Yau construction, there is a unique choice of a Hermitian metric
h
on the line bundle such that the Calabi model potential
\psi_{\mathcal{C}}
defined using
h
(see (
2.65
)) differs from
\psi_{TY_{1}}
by an exponentially decaying term. We shall fix this choice of
h
, i.e., no rescalings of
h
are allowed. Another point that we hope will aid clarity is that we are not allowing any rescalings of
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}
. This also fixes the scale of
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
because
\omega^{2}_{\mathcal{C}}=\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\wedge\overline{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Secondly, we have a family of canonically polarized surfaces
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{3}
and hyperplane sections
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}
with
[2\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}]=K_{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}}
, see (
2.1
) and (
2.5
). We have a family of algebraic holomorphic volume forms
\Omega_{\sigma}
on the affine surfaces
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}=\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}
, which are unique up to scaling (Remark
2.1
). On the regular part of
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
, i.e., on the complement of the origin, we have a Kähler-Einstein metric
\omega_{KE,0}=i\partial\overline{\partial}\psi_{KE,0}
with
e^{-\psi_{KE,0}}\omega^{2}_{KE,0}=\Omega_{0}\wedge\overline{\Omega}_{0}
(Lemma
2.10
). We remark that the additive normalization of
\psi_{KE,0}
depends on the choice of a scale of
\Omega_{0}
, which we will fix in a moment.

Third, we fix a local holomorphic identification
\Psi_{\sigma}
of
TY_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}
, see (
2.9
). By Lemma
2.12
, by a suitable (unique) rescaling of
\Omega_{0}
we can achieve that
(\Psi_{0}^{-1})^{*}(\Omega_{0}\wedge\overline{\Omega}_{0})=(1+O(e^{\frac{t}{2}}))({\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}}\wedge\overline{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}})
as
t=\log h\to-\infty
. We remark that there is no scaling ambiguity for
\Omega_{\sigma}
any more since
\Omega_{0}
is fixed. To get rid of the term
-3\log(1-\mathfrak{s}/t)
in
the expansion of
(\Psi_{0}^{-1})^{*}\psi_{KE,0}-\psi_{cusp}
, we have to compose
\Psi_{\sigma}
with
scale_{e^{-{\mathfrak{s}}}}
, but it is harmless for our purposes to assume directly that
\mathfrak{s}=0
(see Remark
2.15
). Thus we achieve that
(\Psi_{0}^{-1})^{*}\psi_{KE,0}-\psi_{cusp}=O(e^{-\delta_{0}\sqrt{-t}})
as
t\to-\infty
for some
\delta_{0}>0
. Here,
\psi_{cusp}
has already been normalized by adding a constant such that

e^{-\psi_{cusp}}\omega_{cusp}^{2}=\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}\wedge\overline{\Omega}_{\mathcal{C}}
.
The same relation holds for the horn metrics
(\psi_{T},\omega_{T})
of Section
2.4
instead of
(\psi_{cusp},\omega_{cusp})
.

We review the relations between different parameters. The position of the horn is fixed via

\displaystyle T:=\frac{2}{3}\log|\sigma|.

(3.1)

This is motivated by the discussion in Section
1.3
. By (
2.132
), the parameter
b<0
satisfies that
|b|
is uniformly comparable to
|T|^{-3}
. Our preferred radius coordinate
t=\log h
differs from
\log|z|^{2}
by a uniformly bounded function, see again the discussion before (
1.3
). Here,
|z|
is the standard radius in
\mathbb{C}^{3}
, where
TY_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\sigma}
are embedded by definition. Thus,
|\sigma||z|^{-3}
is comparable to
e^{-(3/2)(t-T)}
.

The orange gluing region, where the end of the Tian-Yau space is attached to the left end of the new neck, is parametrized by
t\in[T+T_{0},T+2T_{0}]
(see Figure
2
and Remark
2.22
). Here, as in (
2.176
),

\displaystyle T_{0}:=|T|^{\alpha}\;\,\text{for some fixed}\;\,\alpha\in(0,1).

(3.2)

The limit
\alpha\to 1
corresponds to the naive gluing of the cusp metric and the Tian-Yau metric described after (
1.3
), whereas in this paper we will always fix
\alpha
to be arbitrarily close to zero. It is also worth noting that we do not glue with the Tian-Yau metric normalized exactly as above but, rather, with a scaled copy of it, where the scaling factor
\mathfrak{c}
equals
\sqrt{2}
for
n=2
; see (
2.178
). We could have hidden this factor in our normalization of
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}
, but we chose not to do so because
\mathfrak{c}
appears much later in the paper than
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}
, and a lot of other choices depend on the initial choice of
\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Lastly, the green gluing region between the neck and the cusp is parametrized by
t\in[2\tau,\tau]
(see Remark
2.22
). The only requirement so far was that
\tau/T\to 0
as
\sigma\to 0
. We now fix
\tau
such that

\displaystyle|b||\tau|^{3}=e^{-\delta_{0}\sqrt{-\tau}},

(3.3)

hence in particular
\tau\sim-((3/\delta_{0})\log|T|)^{2}
. This is the classical choice of making the two gluing errors on the left side and on the right side of the gluing region comparable to each other ((
3.11
), line 3).

Table 1.
Properties of
\omega_{glue,\sigma}
. The estimates are sharp up to constant factors.

\mathfrak{R}_{1}

compact part of
\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}

\mathfrak{R}_{2}

hyperbolic cusp

\mathfrak{R}_{3}

green gluing

\mathfrak{R}_{4}

middle neck

Range of
t=\log h

\tau<t<-N

1

\mathfrak{R}_{7}

Tian-Yau cap

(
\alpha\ll 1
)

\gg 1
